# Changelog

This file records user-visible changes before they are committed. New work should
be added here first, then marked `Committed` or `Pushed` after the git action.

- 2026-06-20 20:12:37 +0700 - Document Higgsfield Cloud API Platform Routing
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: Optional Higgsfield generation skill (`packaged-skills/optional/media/higgsfield/higgsfield-generate/SKILL.md`), shared/local skill mirrors, and changelog
  - Summary: The Higgsfield generation skill now records the official Cloud API routing contract from the docs: discover pages through `docs.higgsfield.ai/docs/llms.txt`, submit to `https://platform.higgsfield.ai/{model_id}`, authenticate with `Authorization: Key <api_key>:<api_secret>`, poll/cancel through `/requests/<request_id>`, and use `hf_webhook` for callbacks. The skill also warns that Python `urllib` may be blocked by Cloudflare before auth, that the consumer `higgsfield` CLI/free workspace is not evidence about Cloud API credits, and that Seedance 2.0/Kling 3.0 requests should consult `higgsfield-api-quirks` before building non-standard payloads.
  - Verification: Fetched the official docs index and listed markdown pages; no-cost curl sanity check against the documented platform status endpoint with the shared Higgsfield env reached the platform auth/path layer and returned `404 {"detail":"Not found"}` for a fake request id; shared and local skill mirrors match the packaged skill via `cmp`; focused `git diff --check` passed for the touched repo files. Follow-up mirror checks confirmed the shared/local/packaged Higgsfield generation skill and `references/media-inputs.md` now all include the Seedance 2.0 root-level `input_audio` warning and pass focused `git diff --check`.
  - Intended commit message: `Document Higgsfield Cloud API routing`

- 2026-06-20 10:58:53 +0700 - Add Trade Tab (Crypto And Stocks)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: New Trade navigation view in the left shelf (`src/components/fleet-hive/AppNavShelf.tsx`) and dashboard view plumbing (`src/features/dashboard/dashboard-types.ts`, `dashboard-navigation.ts`, `dashboard-light-helpers.tsx`, `DashboardApp.tsx`, `hooks/use-dashboard-derived-state.tsx`); new Trade panel UI (`src/features/dashboard/views/trade/`) including a wallet-selection modal (`WalletSelectModal.tsx`); reusable selectable compact wallet card (`src/components/wallet/AgentWalletCardCompact.tsx` + `.module.css`); shared configured-wallet check extracted to `src/lib/utils/agent-wallet.ts` and shared personal-wallet loader added to `src/lib/native/personal-wallets.ts`, both reused by `src/features/dashboard/views/WalletPanel.tsx`; the wallet picker now lists the user's own wallets first, then configured agent wallets; side-aware stock trade rail with sell support (`src/lib/services/trading/buy-stock.ts`); new governed stock-trade HTTP route (`src/app/api/trading/route.ts`); agent capability discovery (`src/lib/services/context-index.ts`); wallet/HONEY/x402 docs (`docs/features/wallets-honey-and-x402.md`); auto-pulled Bankr trading wallet (`src/app/api/bankr/wallet/route.ts`); local DEX swap rail — 0x v2/Permit2 EVM primitive in `src/lib/services/wallet/chain-wallet.ts`, swap service `src/lib/services/trading/dex-swap.ts`, route `src/app/api/trading/swap/route.ts`
  - Summary: HivemindOS now has a dedicated Trade tab with a Crypto/Stocks segmented view. Crypto is capability-first — it surfaces every supported action (swap/trade, perps, prediction markets, bridge, token launch, NFT, send, receive, private transfer, paid API, fund LLM credits) with live readiness, shows a clear-signing review on prepare, and executes through the existing hardened provider endpoints via the crypto capability router. Stocks gain a buy AND sell flow over both venues (Alpaca paper/live and on-chain xStocks via Jupiter) through a new governed `/api/trading` route that resolves the acting agent's wallet server-side and enforces `CONFIRM_BUY`/`CONFIRM_SELL`, per-trade caps, and the company kill switch. The acting wallet is chosen through a wallet-selection modal that reuses the Wallets-screen compact card (made selectable) and lists only configured wallets, using the same configured-wallet check the Wallets screen uses. The picker also auto-pulls the Bankr-managed wallet (when the API key is set, with a clear "Bankr-managed" label) and gates Bankr-only intents to it. A local DEX swap rail lets the user trade FROM their own imported/local wallet — 0x v2 (Permit2) on Base and Jupiter on Solana — with a $10 hard cap, spend-governance, a CONFIRM_SWAP gate, and a one-time max Permit2 approval; the older xStocks rail's `dynamicSlippage` flag was dropped because it caused Jupiter 0x1771 (SlippageToleranceExceeded). The Wallets tab is unchanged and still owns accounts, rails, and governance.
  - Verification: `pnpm exec eslint` clean on every touched file; `pnpm tsc` adds ZERO diagnostics in the new/changed files (verified by filtering full `tsc --noEmit` output to those paths). NOTE: repo-wide `pnpm tsc` exits non-zero from ~32 PRE-EXISTING errors unrelated to this change (under `promo-videos/`, `remotion/`, the stale `src-tauri/target/` staged build copy, and `src/features/env/env-components.tsx`); none are in files this change touched. `node scripts/check-file-sizes.mjs` reported all new files under 1500 lines (largest is `buy-stock.ts` at ~430). `node scripts/test-buy-stock-xstocks.mjs` passed: 14 verified xStocks parsed, 14/14 buy-routable on Jupiter, side-aware API + `CONFIRM_SELL` present in source, and the xStock SELL leg (mint→USDC ExactIn) routed live. Runtime smoke test against a local dev server: unauthenticated `GET /api/trading` returns `401 application/json` like its siblings; authenticated `GET /api/trading` returns `200` with `confirmations {buy:CONFIRM_BUY, sell:CONFIRM_SELL}`, 14 xStock tickers, and Alpaca `configured:false` (no keys set). Not verified in this session (the user's to check): the visual rendering of the Trade panel, group-title spacing, and wallet-selection modal; live buy/sell execution, which needs real provider keys and a funded wallet and is irreversible. Known limitation: in packaged static desktop builds the `/api/*` POST paths do not run (same as the existing Wallets send/x402 paths) — execution works in dev and browser builds. DEX rail LIVE-VERIFIED on mainnet within the $10 cap: Base $2 USDC→ETH (approval `0x81818cf9…`, swap `0xa308594e…`) and Solana $2 USDC→SOL (tx `5jQp4z6s…`), both confirmed by on-chain balance deltas; read-only checks confirmed the $10 cap rejects a $15 swap on both chains and the non-stable (SOL/ETH) sell-side pricing path. The first Solana attempt surfaced + fixed the `dynamicSlippage` 0x1771 bug at simulation (no funds moved).
  - Intended commit message: `Add Trade tab with crypto/stock + DEX swap rails (0x + Jupiter)`

- 2026-06-20 09:05:00 +0700 - Add Cloudflare Paid-Agent Gateway Worker
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: Cloudflare paid-agent Worker (`workers/paid-agent-gateway/`), official paid-agent client default (`src/lib/services/paid-agent-cloud-client.ts`), paid-agent worker regression coverage (`scripts/test-paid-agent-worker.mjs`, `scripts/test-paid-agent-gateway.mjs`), wallet/HONEY/x402 docs (`docs/features/wallets-honey-and-x402.md`), context discovery (`src/lib/services/context-index.ts`), and architecture docs (`docs/architecture/api-and-storage.md`, `docs/architecture/index.md`, `docs/index.md`)
  - Summary: HivemindOS now includes a deployed Cloudflare Worker for official x402 paid agents at `https://hivemindos-paid-agent-gateway.hivemindos.workers.dev`. The Worker owns official `payTo` and facilitator configuration, verifies and settles x402 at the edge, stores D1 receipt/idempotency metadata, and forwards paid OpenAI-compatible chat completion requests to a trusted upstream runtime. Downloaded apps now default to that public Worker URL and can override it only for staging, enterprise, or self-hosted official-compatible deployments.
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-paid-agent-worker.mjs` passed with 16 assertions; `node scripts/test-paid-agent-gateway.mjs` passed with 31 assertions; `node --check scripts/test-paid-agent-worker.mjs` passed; `node --check scripts/test-paid-agent-gateway.mjs` passed; `pnpm typecheck` passed inside `workers/paid-agent-gateway` using local ignored dependency symlinks; remote D1 database `hivemindos_paid_agent_gateway` was created and migrated; `wrangler deploy` published Worker version `8bcafc53-4667-424d-be1f-f06cf8399a78`; the upstream URL, upstream bearer, and payTo secrets were uploaded to Cloudflare; live `/health` and paid-agent metadata probes returned configured with no missing keys; an unpaid live POST returned `402 Payment Required` with x402 requirements; focused `git diff --check` passed; `wc -l` confirmed the Worker source, README, and regression test are under 1500 lines.
  - Intended commit message: `Add Cloudflare paid-agent gateway worker`

- 2026-06-20 08:47:58 +0700 - Add Official Hosted Paid-Agent Client Path
  - Status: Uncommitted
  - Areas changed: Official hosted paid-agent proxy (`src/lib/services/paid-agent-cloud-client.ts`, `src/app/api/official-paid-agents/[slug]/chat/completions/route.ts`), x402 paid-agent regression coverage (`scripts/test-paid-agent-gateway.mjs`), capability discovery (`src/lib/services/context-index.ts`), and wallet/HONEY/x402 docs (`docs/features/wallets-honey-and-x402.md`)

- 2026-06-19 18:30:00 +0700 - Agent flows: E2E-validated on the live app + fleet-persistence fix
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: `src/lib/services/queen-bee/flow-runner.ts` (persist fleet snapshot on the run), `scripts/test-agent-flow.mjs`
  - Summary: End-to-end validated the whole agent-flow stack against the running app + the real local Hermes agent. The test surfaced and fixed a real bug: when the autonomous worker auto-advanced a flow, the next node was dispatched without a fleet snapshot (the worker has none of its own), so later tasks were created undelegated and never auto-picked-up — the flow stalled after node 1. Fix: the run now persists the fleet snapshot it started with, and `advanceFlowRun` reuses it, so worker-driven advances keep delegating later nodes. After the fix, a 2-node flow started via the live API self-drove to `done` hands-off: the server worker picked up node 1, the real Hermes agent answered, the auto-advance hook delegated and dispatched node 2, the worker ran it, and the flow completed — with node 1's output threaded into node 2's prompt.
  - Verification: live E2E against http://127.0.0.1:5021 + collector :8787 — (a) flow templates + agent catalog (192) APIs return live data; (b) a research→summary→approve flow ran with the real Hermes agent producing real output at each node, threaded shared state, paused at the HITL approval, and finished `done` after approval; (c) a `sequential`-process company decomposed its goal into a 3-node flow and created a real delegated Work Board task; (d) a 2-node flow self-drove to `done` fully hands-off via the server worker + auto-advance hook. `npm run test:agent-flow` extended with a fleet-persistence assertion and passing; `tsc --noEmit` unchanged at the pre-existing baseline; router/MCP/packaged-agents/autonomous-pickup gates pass.
  - Intended commit message: `Agent flows: persist fleet snapshot for worker-driven advances (E2E-validated)`

- 2026-06-19 17:51:45 +0700 - Stabilize Queen Bee Clap Wake Detection
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: Local clap detector (`src/features/queen-voice/clap-activation.ts`, `src/features/queen-voice/use-queen-clap-activation.ts`), clap regression coverage (`scripts/test-queen-clap-activation.mjs`), assimilation manifest/log (`ASSIMILATION.queen-clap-wake.json`, `ASSIMILATION_LOG.md`, `ASSIMILATION_LOG.jsonl`)
  - Summary: Queen Bee clap wake now follows proven clap/onset detector prior art instead of relying on loudness alone. The listener processes microphone audio through a silent Web Audio processor callback, measures raw sample RMS/peak, computes a spectral-flux onset from analyser frequency bins, and requires broadband high-frequency flux before counting a clap. This keeps two quick real claps responsive while rejecting steady loud audio and low-frequency thumps that were causing random activations.
  - Verification: `pnpm test:queen-clap` passed with 16 checks, including spectral-flux clap onset coverage, low-frequency thump rejection, steady-loud-frame rejection, lower-RMS clap coverage, and local hook lifecycle checks; `pnpm test:queen-echo` passed; focused ESLint passed for touched Queen voice/dashboard settings/clap test files; focused `git diff --check` passed. `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --pretty false` still exits 1 on existing unrelated diagnostics under `promo-videos/`, generated `src-tauri/target/release/resources/`, and `src/features/env/env-components.tsx`; filtered output showed no diagnostics for touched Queen/dashboard clap paths.

  - Summary: Closes the live loop for agent flows. Queen Bee tasks now carry a `source` origin tag ("flow:<runId>:<nodeId>"), and when the autonomous worker completes (or blocks) a flow-tagged task it advances the flow automatically — applying the result, parsing a 0–1 quality score from the node's output for score-gated edges, and dispatching the next node. The hook is a guarded dynamic import so non-flow tasks are untouched and flow advancement can never break task pickup. Adds a Flow Builder UI (Work Board → "Flows"): browse/edit/save flow templates (nodes + conditional edges, including score thresholds and HITL approval nodes), start runs, and approve/reject runs that pause for a human.
  - Verification: `npm run test:agent-flow` extended and passing — covers task-source tag parsing, the stale/double-advance guard, score parsing routing past a quality bar, and a full sequential run driven entirely through `maybeAdvanceFlowForTask` (the worker path). `npm run test:queen-bee:autonomous` still passes (worker contract unchanged). `tsc --noEmit` adds no new errors (FlowBuilderModal and all flow files clean; total unchanged at the pre-existing baseline). Router/MCP/packaged-agents gates pass. NOT runtime-verified: the Flow Builder modal's in-app behavior (typechecked + self-contained, but not smoke-tested in a running app).
  - Intended commit message: `Agent flows: automatic worker-side advancement + flow builder UI`

- 2026-06-19 16:10:00 +0700 - Agent flows: graph/crew orchestration layer for the Queen Bee
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: New flow layer (`src/lib/types/agent-flow.ts`, `src/lib/services/queen-bee/flow-engine.ts`, `flow-templates.ts`, `flow-runner.ts`, `src/app/api/queen-bee/flow/route.ts`); company process (`src/lib/types/company.ts`, `src/lib/services/companies-orchestration.ts`); test (`scripts/test-agent-flow.mjs`, `package.json`)
  - Summary: Adds the missing composition layer that turns HivemindOS's existing crew (Company) + single-node loop (KanbanLoopSpec) into LangGraph/CrewAI-style flows. A FlowSpec is nodes (worker-class steps or human-approval checkpoints) wired by conditional edges (success/failure/score thresholds), with a shared state object threaded through each step. A pure engine evaluates edges, loops back bounded by a step cap + per-node retry, pauses for human-in-the-loop approval, and terminates on DONE/FAIL — modelling research → draft → (quality < bar ? back to research : publish) → approve. A runner submits each node's task via the Queen Bee (reusing routing/receipts) and advances when a node completes. Companies gain a `process` field: "hierarchical" (default, unchanged parallel fan-out), "sequential" (CrewAI-style ordered chain, each step consuming the prior output), or "graph" (run a saved FlowSpec). Built-in templates ship and user flows persist to the vault under Operations/Flows. New API: GET/POST `/api/queen-bee/flow` (list/get templates + runs, save-template, start, advance, resolve-approval).
  - Verification: `npm run test:agent-flow` passes — exercises the branch+bounded-loop+HITL flow (research runs twice on a sub-bar score, then publishes and waits for approval), the step-cap killing an infinite loop, approval-rejection routing to FAIL, the sequential builder + {{state}}/{{last}} prompt rendering, and the runner driving a full run via injected mock dispatch with vault persistence. `tsc --noEmit` adds no new errors (total unchanged at the pre-existing baseline). Router/MCP/packaged-agents gates still pass. The default `hierarchical` company path is untouched (guarded behind the new `process` check). NOT done / follow-up: automatic worker-side advancement (today the executor advances via the POST advance API; the autonomous worker was left untouched to avoid destabilizing the live loop, since task source is carried in the task body not a field), and a flow-builder UI.
  - Intended commit message: `Agent flows: conditional-graph + crew orchestration for the Queen Bee`

The encrypted wallet vault serializes read-modify-write updates, writes atomically, and can recover from concatenated vault snapshots instead of treating the local signer vault as empty. This prevents a reimport from briefly showing both chains as spendable while a reload later downgrades Base back to view-only.
  - Verification: `pnpm test:wallet-vault` passed; `pnpm test:hive-staking` passed with 22 assertions; focused ESLint passed for the touched wallet-vault, import-route, staking, and test files; focused `git diff --check` passed; authenticated local API check confirmed the current persisted state is Solana local and Base watch because the old race already lost the Base signer record. Full `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --pretty false --skipLibCheck` still exits on unrelated existing diagnostics under `promo-videos/`, `remotion/`, and generated `src-tauri/target/...`, with no touched-file diagnostics reported.
  - Intended commit message: `Preserve recovery phrase wallet signers`

  - Summary: Shared Brain Memory can now evolve stale durable memories instead of piling up contradictions. The new `evolve` action writes a fresh active memory, marks older Agent Memory notes as superseded, records `supersedes`/`supersededBy`/`evolutionRootId` chain metadata, and returns evolution history during recall/answer so agents treat the latest active version as current truth while preserving prior versions as evidence. `hive-brain evolve` exposes the same update path for raw agents when the app API is reachable, managed chat context explains how to read evolved memory chains, the new auto-installed `hive-brain-memory` skill teaches agents recall/remember/evolve policy, and full-vault fallback recall now skips stale generated index rows that point at missing notes.

  - Summary: Fixes provider model-count issues. (1) Configured providers showed wrong/low counts — OpenAI "0 models", and Venice/UsePod only their Hermes-configured subset (e.g. 4/2) rather than the provider's full catalog. A new auth-gated `/api/providers/models` route fetches a provider's live `/models` using the hive-env key (never returned to the client): catalog entries carry a base URL + auth style (OpenAI/Anthropic/Groq/Gemini/Venice); Venice-style non-chat models (image/audio/embedding) are filtered out. (OpenRouter is excluded — huge catalog, reached via Adaptive/inventory.) (2) The settings status sweep ran Bankr model discovery, Bankr access status, and LM Studio discovery sequentially on every refresh; these independent probes now run concurrently (`Promise.all`), so the sweep waits the slowest probe instead of their sum. (The remaining ~6.6s is the Hermes inventory python, inherent to the Hermes runtime.) (3) Gateways seed a single placeholder `defaultModel`, so tiles briefly read a misleading "1 model" before the sweep filled in the real count; gateway tiles now show "Loading models" while the sweep is in flight and snap to the real count once (Hive Fusion's stable count and the catalog "Key set" tiles are excluded).

Live `/models` fetch depends on outbound network to the provider; it degrades gracefully (route 502 → tile stays "Key set", user can Add model manually).

- 2026-06-14 02:05:00 +0700 - Add Hive Fusion Compound Model
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new Fusion orchestrator (`src/lib/services/fusion/{types,catalog,prompts,client,orchestrator,route-stream}.ts`), chat route intercept (`src/app/api/chat/agent-runtime/route.ts`), provider/model registry (`src/lib/config/model-provider-gateways.ts`), agent profile types (`src/lib/types/agent-runtime.ts`), model picker (`src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-agent-settings-controller.tsx`), provider icons (`public/icons/runtimes/hive-fusion.svg`, `public/icons/runtimes/openrouter.svg`), test + loader (`scripts/test-fusion.mjs`, `scripts/lib/ts-relative-loader.mjs`), docs (`docs/features/runtimes-and-chat.md`)
  - Summary: Implements Hive Fusion, a native compound model. Selecting the `Hive Fusion` provider (available on any runtime) fans a prompt out to a panel of the configured providers (OpenRouter free-budget models, Venice, OpenAI, Groq, Bankr, or a local OpenAI-compatible server) in parallel, runs a judge model to extract consensus/contradictions/coverage/unique-insights/blind-spots, then streams a synthesizer answer grounded in that analysis. The panel auto-resolves from whichever provider keys are present and degrades gracefully to a low-diversity single-member panel; `agent.fusion` overrides participants/judge/synthesizer/mode (mirroring OpenRouter's custom-panel option). OpenRouter's hosted compound model is surfaced separately as the `openrouter/fusion` slug under the OpenRouter provider (served by the normal OpenRouter path). The chat send path is unchanged — the route detects `provider: "hive-fusion"` and delegates before the single-model preflight, emitting panel/judge progress as tool/reasoning events and the synthesizer as the streamed answer over the existing SSE contract. Fusion output is run through the same `proxyOutput`/`redactSecretText` security proxy as every other runtime path, streamed synthesis uses an idle (not fixed) timeout, and selecting Fusion never writes a Hermes/OpenClaw gateway model (client guard + server-side set-model guard).
  - Verification: `node scripts/test-fusion.mjs` passed (36 assertions across the happy path, partial/all-member failure, synth fallback incl. mid-stream interruption, single-success judge-skip, judge failure, hosted OpenRouter mode, and the judge-JSON parser); `npx tsc --noEmit` reported 0 errors under `src/`; `npx eslint` reported 0 problems on all touched files. A 4-dimension adversarial multi-agent review (correctness/stream-contract/integration/security) confirmed and fixed 9 issues (2 high: OpenRouter `plugins` body shape + output-proxy bypass) and dismissed 7 non-issues. The orchestrator runs offline in tests via injected provider callers plus a `.ts`-extension resolve hook.

  - Summary: Adds a HivemindOS-native compiled-brain workflow inspired by The Curator. Agents can now compile important source material, research findings, and chat conclusions into Obsidian entity/concept/summary wiki pages under `Synthesis/Compiled Knowledge/<domain>/`, search that compiled wiki with weighted slug/title/tag/body matching, query it as a graph, inspect backlinks, scan wiki health, apply safe repairs, and consult a shared-brain contribution contract. Human collective `shared-*` mirrors follow the personal-domain-then-synthesis model, while normal agent-to-agent HivemindOS contributions remain permissive unless a domain is explicitly read-only.
  - Verification: `pnpm test:compiled-knowledge` passed and covers compiled wiki writes, compiled search, graph overview/full graph reads, node/backlink lookup, health scan/dismissal, MCP tool exposure, packaged skill discovery, and the shared contribution contract (`human-collective` protects `shared-*`; `agent-to-agent` stays permissive). `pnpm benchmark:compiled-knowledge` passed on a synthetic 720-page compiled-knowledge vault across 15 runs: old generic graph median 136.48ms with 271,902-byte payload and only 260 real notes/520 links before truncation; new compiled overview median 39.45ms with 2,851-byte payload while covering 720 notes/1,440 edges (3.46x faster, 95.37x smaller, 2.77x more real-note and edge coverage); new full compiled graph median 36.56ms while returning all 720 notes/1,440 edges. Direct slug lookup for `concept-120` improved from broad-graph scan median 135.81ms/271,902 bytes to compiled node lookup median 31.71ms/392 bytes (4.28x faster, 693.63x smaller). Backlink lookup improved from broad-graph filter median 87.25ms/271,902 bytes and 0 backlinks because of truncation to compiled backlink lookup median 32.44ms/80 bytes and 2 backlinks (2.69x faster, 3,398.78x smaller, more complete). New compiled search for `Concept 120 benchmark graph` improved from broad vault rg markdown search median 128.67ms/3,465 bytes to compiled search median 67.18ms/3,377 bytes with the same top target (`concept-120`), giving 1.92x faster search and a 1.03x smaller payload. Health scan has no old generic equivalent; the new compiled health scan completed at median 62.47ms with a 220-byte clean-health payload. Live shared-vault discovery was refreshed with `./scripts/seed-shared-skills.sh --import-sources none --share-targets none`; `rg` found `brain_search_knowledge` in `Skills/README.md`, `Skills/hive-brain-compiled-wiki/SKILL.md`, and shared vault `AGENTS.md`; `hive-brain recall "hive-brain-compiled-wiki compile_brain_knowledge brain_get_backlinks" --scope full-vault --limit 8` returned `Hive Brain Compiled Wiki` as the top hit; and `node scripts/hivemind-mcp` advertised `brain_search_knowledge` plus the existing compiled-brain tools. `node --check scripts/hivemind-mcp scripts/test-compiled-knowledge.mjs scripts/benchmark-compiled-knowledge.mjs` passed; focused `pnpm exec eslint` passed for the touched compiled-brain service, API route, context index, shared-vault context, and scripts; filtered `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` output has no diagnostics for the touched compiled-search files; `git diff --check` passed. `node scripts/check-file-sizes.mjs` still fails on pre-existing oversized files, and the touched files are under 1500 lines. `pnpm test:vault-structure` is still blocked by an existing docs layout assertion (`docs/_layouts/default.html` missing `/whole-brain/`).

  - Verification: `pnpm exec eslint src/lib/services/obsidian/okf.ts src/app/api/brain/okf/route.ts scripts/test-okf-export.mjs --max-warnings=0` passed; `pnpm test:okf-export` passed; `pnpm test:vault-structure` passed; filtered `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --pretty false` returned no diagnostics for the OKF service, route, or test script; full `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --pretty false` remains blocked by pre-existing unrelated diagnostics in `remotion/gitlawb-integration-video/src/GitLawbIntegrationVideo.tsx` (`pathLength`) and stale Tauri release resources missing the `security` bee worker class. Live API smoke on temporary dev server `http://127.0.0.1:5021` exported 12 Agent Memory concepts to `Operations/Brain Services/OKF Export` and validation returned `ok: true`, `errors: 0`, `warnings: 0`; direct validation GET returned the same. Generated OKF export was scanned for raw IP-looking strings and obvious secret tokens; hits were proof hashes, credential key names, and loopback/local references, not raw Tailnet IPs or plaintext secret values. `git diff --check` passed for touched files.
  - Intended commit message: `Add OKF export and validation for shared brain`

- 2026-06-14 03:30:00 +0700 - Fix Windows/Linux machines missing from Fleet + add boot-failure recovery
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: tailnet device filter (`src/app/api/tailscale/devices/route.ts`, `src/app/api/fleet/discover/route.ts`), client self re-injection (`src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx`), interactive boot-error screen + retry + 60s timeout (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `src-tauri/loading-shell/index.html`, `src-tauri/build.rs`, `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`)
  - Summary: ROOT CAUSE for a Windows desktop showing a completely empty Fleet (no machine cell, no add-agent cell): the fleet device list filtered devices to mac/mobile/hivemind-link only (`dedupeDevices` in both the tailscale-devices and fleet-discover routes), so a Windows or Linux machine — including the user's own self device — was dropped entirely, and any agents on it had no machine cell to render in. Both routes now keep Windows/Linux desktops and never drop a machine's own `self` device. A client-side safety net also re-injects the native self device if any upstream source omits it. SEPARATELY, boot resilience: when the embedded server fails to start, the window now shows an interactive error screen (server-log tail + one-click Copy + a real Retry that re-spawns the server without restarting the app — the old "rerun setup" did nothing), and the failure is reported to telemetry from the webview (the static error page reported nothing, which is why these failures were invisible). The first-boot wait is raised 25s -> 60s for slow Defender-scanned Windows first launches.
  - Verification: `cargo check --release`; `pnpm check:tauri-acl` (38 commands in lockstep); `pnpm exec eslint` clean on changed TS. Live confirmation pending on the affected Windows machine.
  - Intended commit message: `Fix Windows/Linux fleet visibility + add boot-failure recovery screen`

- 2026-06-14 02:10:00 +0700 - Fix empty Fleet view on a fresh install (self machine never showed)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: tailnet device handling on the dashboard (`src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx` `refreshTailscaleDevices`)
  - Summary: Fixes a brand-new install (e.g. Windows, no Tailscale, no agents) showing an empty Fleet view — no "This Mac" self cell and no "add agent" cell, just a perpetual loading swarm with no way to onboard a first agent. The native fleet read correctly returns the local self device, but the client's `localOnlyStatusFallback` guard (meant to avoid clobbering an existing multi-machine fleet during a momentary Tailscale outage) was discarding that self device on a fresh machine that has no Tailscale at all. With zero machines the hive renders only its loading skeleton, and the add-agent cell never appears because it lives inside a machine cluster. Now the guard only suppresses the self-only update when a fleet already exists; on a fresh box it surfaces the self machine, so the Fleet view always shows "This Mac" plus the add-agent affordance.
  - Verification: traced the full render path (FleetView -> NetworkGraph -> MachineCluster add-hex-cell; machineGroups -> fleetViewData filter, which passes any `machine.self`); confirmed the self device passes the filter once set. `pnpm exec eslint` on the changed file. Live fresh-install fleet to be confirmed on the affected Windows machine after the build.
  - Intended commit message: `Fix empty fleet on fresh install: surface self machine when no prior fleet`

- 2026-06-14 01:30:00 +0700 - Fix Windows: embedded server never started (EISDIR on drive root)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: Node spawn flag in the embedded boot (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs` `start_native_next_server`); removed the one-off `\.github/workflows/debug-windows-server-boot.yml` diagnostic
  - Summary: Fixes the Windows-only "HivemindOS couldn't start its local server" error that hit every Windows install since the first embedded release (v0.2.10). The Tauri runtime hands Node the embedded server path in Windows extended-length (verbatim) form (`\\?\C:\...\server.js`); Node realpath-resolves the main module at startup and crashes on that form with `EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, lstat 'C:'`, so the dashboard backend never booted and every launch (and "rerun setup") landed on the error page. The fix launches Node with `--preserve-symlinks-main`, which skips the realpath of the entry script while keeping the long-path-safe prefix. macOS/Linux are unaffected.
  - Verification: Reproduced AND fix-validated on a windows-latest runner: the verbatim path crashes with the exact user error, and the same path with `--preserve-symlinks-main` boots and serves (SSR + /api). `cargo check --release` passes. NOTE: a fully-broken Windows install can't auto-update (the updater runs in the dashboard, which never loaded), so affected users must download the fixed build once.
  - Intended commit message: `Fix Windows embedded server boot: --preserve-symlinks-main (EISDIR on \\?\ path)`

- 2026-06-14 00:30:00 +0700 - Instant-paint launch for the embedded desktop app
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: async embedded boot + navigate-on-ready (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), branded loading shell (`src-tauri/loading-shell/index.html`), ship shell as embedded frontendDist (`scripts/tauri-build.mjs`), expose the Tauri bridge to the shell (`src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` `withGlobalTauri`)
  - Summary: The desktop app now paints instantly at launch instead of showing a frozen blank window for 1-3s while its local server boots. Previously the embedded build spawned the bundled Node server synchronously inside Tauri's setup() on the main thread, freezing the whole app (no paint) until the server's port opened. Now the server boots on a background thread and the window immediately shows a branded HivemindOS loading screen served from a local file — which reads live data from the native Rust commands (This Mac + how many tailnet machines are online) so the first thing you see is real, not just a spinner. When the server is ready the window swaps to the full dashboard automatically; if it never comes up, the existing local-server error page is shown. Everything degrades gracefully: if a native read is unavailable the stat is hidden and the branded loader still shows.
  - Verification: `cargo check --release` (the boot path is release-only); `node --check scripts/tauri-build.mjs`; `pnpm check:tauri-acl` (36 commands in lockstep); JSON config validated. True first-paint timing is a GUI behavior — to be confirmed on a desktop build/launch (the shell + async boot are structurally verified in the bundle).
  - Intended commit message: `Instant-paint launch: async embedded boot + native loading shell`

- 2026-06-13 14:49:41 +0700 - Add Buy-Stock Rail (Alpaca + On-Chain xStocks)
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: new buy-stock service (`src/lib/services/trading/buy-stock.ts`), verified xStocks mint allowlist (`src/lib/config/xstocks-tokens.ts`), wallet types + spend ledger (`src/lib/types/agent-wallet.ts`, `src/lib/services/wallet/spend-ledger.ts`), agent chat runtime wiring (`src/app/api/chat/agent-runtime/route.ts`), wallet config UI (`src/components/wallet/AgentWalletCard.tsx`), tests (`scripts/test-buy-stock-xstocks.mjs`, `scripts/e2e-buy-stock-alpaca.mjs`, `scripts/e2e-buy-stock-xstocks-swap.mjs`, `package.json`), docs (`docs/features/wallets-honey-and-x402.md`, `docs/features/index.md`)
  - Summary: Adds a unified "buy a stock from a prompt" rail with two venues behind one tool. `alpaca` places market orders through the Alpaca Trading API (paper by default; live only when the wallet sets `alpacaPaper:false`; keys read from shared hive env by name, never stored in project files). `xstocks` buys on-chain tokenized equities by swapping USDC into a verified xStock SPL token via Jupiter, signed by the agent's existing local Solana wallet. Tickers resolve only through a hard-coded Jupiter-verified mint allowlist (every mint carries the official `Xs` vanity prefix) so Solana symbol-squatting copycats cannot be bought. The flow reuses the existing draft -> confirm -> execute chat pattern ("buy $25 of AAPL on xstocks" -> Buy stock ready card -> reply `confirm`), a per-trade USD cap (`maxTradeUsd`), and the shared spend-governance chokepoint + ledger (a new `trade` spend kind), so kill switches, rolling budgets, and approval escalation all apply. The Wallets tab gets a Stock buying section (venue, Alpaca paper/live, max per trade).
  - Verification: `tsc` and `eslint` clean on all new/changed source files (the only remaining `tsc` errors are pre-existing and unrelated: a Remotion `pathLength` error and stale copies under `src-tauri/target/release/resources/`). `pnpm test:buy-stock` passed (14/14 verified xStock mints structurally sound and live-routable on Jupiter). On-chain xStocks E2E verified with real funds: a $1.00 USDC -> 0.00343601 AAPLx swap confirmed on Solana mainnet (tx `4QHtuZza1jikX25xkbNNdpHTP5Y8C2YDExCU8yvmK4CESwuXcagwN1HEEqpAPoj134PeS2GTypWAqdJiCJYHrNN`), with before/after balances read back to prove settlement. Alpaca paper E2E (`pnpm e2e:buy-stock-alpaca`) is written and ready but not yet run: no `ALPACA_*` keys are present in the shared hive env.
  - Intended commit message: `Add buy-stock rail: Alpaca brokerage + on-chain xStocks`

- 2026-06-13 11:03:47 +0700 - Fix Brain View Empty Boot Root Cause
  - Status: Pushed
  - Areas changed: dashboard state hydration (`src/lib/services/dashboard-state-client.ts`, `scripts/test-dashboard-state-snapshot.mjs`), Brain graph API/native builders (`src/lib/services/obsidian/brain-graph.ts`, `src-tauri/src/brain.rs`), Brain graph refresh and inspector UI (`src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-miroshark-brain-controller.tsx`, `src/features/dashboard/views/BrainGraphExplorer.tsx`)
  - Summary: Fixes the Brain view empty-boot root cause: an expired or missing dashboard auth session could make `/api/dashboard/state` return `401`, and the client treated that auth denial as a legitimate empty dashboard snapshot. That let an already-mounted dashboard hydrate into a fake-empty Brain state with 0 notes instead of returning to the unlock flow. The snapshot loader now treats auth denial as auth denial, never `{}`, and a regression test locks that behavior. As secondary guardrails, the Brain graph loader refuses to accept an empty native graph without trying the API graph builder, both graph builders now emit one edge per source/target pair, the inspector dedupes linked-node lists before rendering buttons, and the native scanner reports folder read failures instead of silently turning them into an empty graph.
  - Verification: `pnpm test:dashboard-state-snapshot` passed; `cargo check` passed for the Tauri native scanner; `pnpm exec eslint src/lib/services/dashboard-state-client.ts src/lib/services/obsidian/brain-graph.ts src/features/dashboard/views/BrainGraphExplorer.tsx src/features/dashboard/hooks/use-miroshark-brain-controller.tsx` reports no errors, only pre-existing hook dependency warnings in the large controller; live `localhost:5121` checks show `/api/dashboard/state` returns `401` when unauthenticated while `/api/obsidian/graph` can still return the populated vault graph (`267` nodes and `11` deduped links, no duplicate edge pairs), confirming the graph endpoint was not the empty-boot root failure and the duplicate React key warning path is removed. Full `pnpm typecheck` is blocked by an unrelated existing Remotion error in `remotion/gitlawb-integration-video/src/GitLawbIntegrationVideo.tsx` (`pathLength` is not a valid CSS property).
  - Intended commit message: `Fix Brain view auth-denied empty boot`

## 2026-06-13 10:52:28 +0700 - Fix Stuck Dashboard Loading Screen And Add Issue Reporting

- Status: Pushed
- Areas changed: dashboard hydration loader (`src/lib/services/dashboard-state-client.ts`, `src/features/dashboard/DashboardApp.tsx`), new client issue reporter (`src/lib/utils/issue-reporter.ts`), new Cloudflare Worker (`workers/issues/`)
- Summary: Fixes the bug where a fresh install could sit forever on a loading screen ("Scanning fleet discovery" on the desktop, "Opening Agent Chat" on mobile). Both screens gate on dashboard hydration, which only completed after the local-state snapshot loaded — and that loader retried `/api/dashboard/state` indefinitely with no give-up, so any persistent backend failure left the app on an endless spinner. The retry is preserved (it protects stored chat history from an empty-boot overwrite), but after ~10s of failure the app now shows a recoverable "Still connecting to HivemindOS…" screen with a Reload button that clears itself the moment the service answers. Broken boots are also reported (anonymized: failure kind, HTTP status, app version, coarse UA, random per-install id — never user content, paths, secrets, or IPs) to a new `hivemindos-issues` Cloudflare Worker + D1, so a stuck install is visible centrally without asking the user to open devtools.
- Verification: `tsc`/`eslint` clean on all changed files; `pnpm test:dashboard-state-snapshot` green (no-data-loss semantics preserved); issues Worker deployed and round-trip verified live (POST accepted, token-gated read works, unauthorized → 401). The loading screen itself is Tauri-only and needs a desktop build for a live look.
- Intended commit message: `Fix dashboard loading-screen hang; add anonymized issue reporting`
